It has three names on it and his own at the foot of the third sheet in his own hand, and it is a thing any clerk in that building can ask for and be handed and copy and carry to a man on a shore he has never stood on. He knew all of that on the day he tied the string round it and he knows it now, and it will be just as true on the day a man he will never meet asks for it, and there is no arrangement of this in which it is not.** A penny exercise book was in the inside pocket of his coat with six torn-out pages in it, and the sixth of them had a date on it and the words that it was his and a reason on it, and the reason was the one he could have put his hand up to in a room, and it was not the reason, and he had put the page back in with the other five, **and that was the whole of what had happened to him in fifty-nine days.**

**The right of refusal is not restored. It was not restored on this coast and it is not restored by a piece of paper about a wall, and whether it ever was is a question about four hundred years and about a quarter of a sentence that somebody entered a long time ago, which is four hundred miles behind him and not on this coast, and nobody on this coast is going to answer it and nothing on this coast was offered to anybody as its answer.** And a woman of twenty-four went back to a second fire in a shed two miles along a coast and put a bar into it and stood at it, because a second fire cannot be walked away from once it is let in, and she stands at it about six days in seven. **And there is a pane on the wall above a bench, three years old, whose third column gives the outer end of that wall as a place, two hundred and sixty-one yards from the post at the inner end, and whose eighth column says the toe stands to the second course for the whole length and to the fifth at the inner end; and there is a pane in a room a mile up this hill whose third column says two hundred and sixty-four and whose eighth says the fourth course for the whole length and does not give the inner end at all; and there is a row of four hundred years of weeks along a wall with gaps in it where nothing was kept; and there are nine uneven columns and a row of eleven marks at the bottom of it; and a man of thirty-one who knows what they are and has told nobody and is going to walk down a coast road tomorrow.**

**And the last thing on the page is a person doing their work.** The sixth crossing went over. A boy of eleven went over at a penny with a hand-lead in his fist, and a woman of forty-eight wrote it on a slate on her knee under her awning at Wether Cross and was paid nothing and had never once said so, and the chain came up and went into its sockets, and the sea was down on that bar and going out long and soft over about two hundred yards of gravel, the way it does four days in seven and not on the other three, **and nobody on that flat thought anything about any of it.**

## Chapter 0001 — The Route That Remembered

Ilyan finds that *The Sovereign’s Wake* erases the cause of a refusal while marking the route complete. He files a precise, unmerged defect report despite the cancellation and a project lead who calls the problem noise. A signed sequence activates a separate one-use null passage without consent. Ilyan lands beneath Orison’s ninth bridge with a spent blackglass token as its reserve and existing drift worsen; Tamsin’s hand appears under the failing span.

## Chapter 0002 — The Current Beneath the Bridge

Ilyan cannot understand ambient Veyran speech, but the formal case notice names him First Defendant and offers three costless-looking remedies. Tarin interprets the human warning; Oren explains the bridge’s count and load marks. Ilyan refuses the abstract choice, identifies the child and her need for a safe exit, and reaches the anchor plate as the span folds toward Tamsin.

## Chapter 0003 — The Brace That Could Be Repeated

Ilyan asks Oren and Tarin for a controlled rescue instead of pretending he can order the bridge. A clamp, cord, and drive pin create a repeatable pocket; Tamsin warns him not to pull and chooses to hear the bridge rather than be promised an uncertain rescue. Neris arrives, names the household’s access and work needs, refuses public use of the private flood account, and the court notice demands five challenge fields before the next bell, which transfers the filing into the midday session.

## Chapter 0004 — The Form of a Challenge

In the Chamber of Passage, Sera Quill accepts provisional representation for the record rather than Ilyan’s innocence. Harrow distinguishes review, appeal, and exit, and accepts Ilyan’s challenge filing without granting an access exception. Ilyan volunteers for a bounded public witness duty that ends with the first field; his report and identity become public. Oren produces a saltglass workroom plate whose maintenance-review language differs from the current charter.

## Chapter 0005 — The Useful Wording

Oren obtains a one-bell maintenance exception for his named crew and one load path. Ilyan turns the inspection into a repeatable sequence and sees the old review condition, but an eastern petition copies its language into a dangerous claim about unlisted refusal. At the deadline, Ilyan files Neris’s private flood account without asking. The Bench preserves the copy, Sera halts the access request and suspends representation, and the household must decide what can be offered after the breach.

## Chapter 0006 — The Name of the Bell

Ilyan explains the preserved copy, the withdrawal mark, and the limits of a court’s ability to restore privacy. Neris says the old strict register once felt protective during an earlier flood before a local petition made it exclusionary. Tamsin chooses to offer only that the warning bell rang before the collapse; Neris refuses the flood history. Sera agrees to advocate the household’s limited request while Ilyan’s representation remains suspended, and the one-bell night field forms.

## Chapter 0007 — The Field Around the Bridge

The first night field tests the current charter, the maintenance record, and the bridge’s behavior. Harrow uses the Bench’s technical term “playable,” while a continuity line appears beneath the formal notice. Ilyan and Tarin establish a neutral perimeter and keep the household at the boundary. Harrow physically replaces the old working plate with the current certified duplicate; the original is sealed, the field destabilizes, and the bridge survives on its brace with the missing language absent from the active record.

## Chapter 0008 — The Right to Say No

Sera confronts Ilyan in her room and makes him record the breach, apologize without asking forgiveness, and accept that his future claims will be public. The court copy is marked disputed, the people named in the account are not treated as consenting witnesses, and Neris retains the original. Sera identifies the surviving phrase “the governed may answer no,” finds her private duplicate of the Quiet Chamber page missing after the night field, and resumes only limited provisional representation of Ilyan after the correction.

## Chapter 0009 — The Record That Walked

The guild posts a safety order, the eastern district invokes its mounted petition, and the crowd arrives. Sera translates the household’s choices and calls Neris and Tamsin without making them. When the clerk physically couples the petition to the bridge record, competing registered and unlisted conditions move through the span. Ilyan proposes one public review with separate burdens and refuses to treat copied wording as household consent. The preliminary hearing opens with amendment, destruction, and delay before the Bench.

## Chapter 0010 — The Stay

Tamsin offers only the bell fact, Neris offers register/work/housing facts and refuses the flood account, and Oren offers the bridge’s physical sequence without accepting unlimited guild liability. Ilyan names a finite public burden attached to his identity and report through the review. The Bench grants limited provisional standing and a one-cycle stay with a public seventh-morning-bell review; the current charter remains untouched. Tamsin and Neris cross together and the repaired load holds. Sera files a licensing recommendation, then distinguishes the original Quiet Chamber page’s pre-arrival removal from the later disappearance of her duplicate and takes Ilyan to the archive.

## Chapter 0011 — The Word That Traveled

A Merefen courier brings an extract that detaches the ninth-bridge stay from its named bridge, crew, burden, and review date. Ilyan and Sera discover that a local clerk may lawfully reuse public wording as a precedent witness without asking the original parties. Ilyan chooses a bounded review rather than a freeze that could close the road and water route, accepting that his name will travel. Neris and Tamsin keep the Ro household’s safety, work, housing, and consent boundaries visible. The extract’s added sentence reads, “the unlisted ward may refuse at the cost of the shared road.” The ward is known only as the unlisted ward; the name Cinder Ward does not appear until Chapter 12.

## Chapter 0012 — The Road That Borrowed the Bridge

Ilyan observes the Saltwheel Road’s carts, water route, load marks, and levy. A loaded cart exposes a hollow seam and cracks the crossing-side marker; Joren says the course has not gone through and that one heavier wheel will finish it. The breach itself is saved for Chapter 14. The Cinder Ward is divided: some residents need the road, some cannot pay the levy, and many have no recognized address for notice. Joren Vell identifies the physical failure and refuses to sign a clean inspection without ward representation. He offers the road as a witness but rejects Ilyan’s plan, turning the local dispute toward the Bench.

## Chapter 0013 — A Name in the Margin

At a record shop, Ilyan and Sera find that the Merefen petition combines older road clauses and the copied ninth-bridge wording. It was entered as a precedent witness, not a ruling, but a marshal has already used it to justify a maintenance levy. Sera refuses to hide the acceptance ledger from the ward. The Bench grants preservation of the ledger and refuses the hold on enforcement, so the batch’s first filing returns worse than it went in: the record is protected and the road is not. The ledger’s final witness entry is removed, leaving a Kest fragment and a clipped continuity-cadence replacement.

## Chapter 0014 — Tarin’s Boundary

Tarin imposes a neutral route and public witness list after a containment order. Ilyan documents the inspection rather than directing the crowd. A loaded cart breaks the crossing-side marker and shifts the water route. Beneath the district’s newer plate, the marker carries private wording closely matching Ilyan’s Earth report: “Completion does not erase the cause of refusal.” Tarin secures the physical evidence and warns Ilyan not to request a broad emergency.

## Chapter 0015 — A Good Exception, a Bad Road

At the Cinder Ward, Mava Rusk refuses a maintenance acknowledgment and loses water and shelter priority. The district invokes Ilyan’s stay as settled precedent. Ilyan states that the stay was bounded by one bridge, a named crew, a household, and a review date; the copied petition has removed those conditions. The refusal triggers a road-wide closure, but ward residents use a slower route to deliver clinic herbs. A petition-box fragment survives with the words, “A burden may be refused when its effect is made visible,” while its lower half is cleanly cut away.

## Chapter 0016 — Recovery Work

Oren, Joren, the ward workers, and the district repair the Saltwheel Road without pretending bridge knowledge transfers automatically. The local keeper and residents name the work, hours, materials, warning line, water route, review, and exit. Ilyan documents the sequence only. The road opens under a finite local arrangement, but the district refuses to publish the ward’s refusal. A proposed renewal instruction arrives before the first interval ends; it has no end date and will become active only when entered.

## Chapter 0017 — The Assembly Refuses a Clean Answer

The Merefen assembly splits between road users, ward residents, and districts afraid that one unlisted ward will become a universal precedent. Ilyan refuses to offer a universal declaration and supports a visible, specific refusal. He submits his public Earth sequence for neutral comparison without accepting authorship. The comparison sets three dates side by side and finds that the local petition was built from the public wording before the Quiet Chamber margin was rewritten. The copier and the person who tidied the record afterward are not the same person. Maren Kett’s written mandate is read into the hall before he argues.

## Chapter 0018 — The Family Appeal

Sera keeps her family’s withdrawal claim separate from the road case and accepts Ilyan only as a record-checker. The original page’s removal predates Ilyan’s crossing; her duplicate vanished during or after the first field. A formal continuity request has a valid seal but no named author, a C—2 code, and a missing witness index. Ilyan admits he cannot infer guilt from the seal and agrees to trace the ordinary chain rather than hunt a villain.

## Chapter 0019 — The Copy Reaches the Road

A district clerk from Merefen enters the automatic-renewal order into the active petition book at a witnessed hour, before anyone can stop it, and the entered record treats the ward’s work as consent and threatens a pre-dawn closure. Ilyan and Sera frame a narrow challenge, while Joren, Dalla, and Mava each choose limited testimony. Tarin keeps the line neutral and refuses force. The Bench pauses automatic renewal, seals rather than erases the copied petition, and keeps the local route available for a short interval. A second paper using Ilyan’s private Earth sequence enters the court slot.

## Chapter 0020 — The Precedent Has a Body

The district offers to reopen the road if Mava signs, but she refuses the acknowledgment and accepts only the named work and public review. The ward’s refusal and labor are entered in the common register. At the morning bell, the affected people choose a fresh one-interval continuation, so the route remains open without becoming permanent precedent. Ilyan claims no authorship or general authority. Sera links the second paper to the C—2 continuity request and finds that the old bridge plate bears an S. Kest fragment; the paper’s receipt predates Ilyan’s arrival, leaving the question of how his private words entered Veyran law.

## Chapter 0021 — The Guild Counts the Work

Oren counts the guild's work in hours and posts a notice naming the ninth-bridge work, the hands, the wage, and Ilyan as attached record with no maintenance labor assigned. Maren Kett arrives with a written mandate and argues that the stay has become a regional obligation, and that the region cannot pay for an inspection nobody requires. A new inspection exposes the missing term with teeth: under the current charter a keeper may summon a pause but may not release the span, so Oren can ring a bell that means nothing. The notice's accurate last line is read by the district as proof that nobody carries the maintenance, and the absence becomes the day's danger.

## Chapter 0022 — A House at the Edge

The household's shelter, room, work hour, and crossing hours are separated by Ilyan and Sera into four records with four recipients, which Meridith Vale immediately explains means four doors where there was one locked gate. The district proposes a three-day materials store on the ground that a provisional tenancy has no tenancy to protect. Tamsin is invited to repeat the bell for the assembly and chooses to say it once in the workroom, where the span is visible while she talks about it, because otherwise people will say the bell was working for her and not for the keeper who rings it. Neris offers route facts only. A work record shows the Ro household's crossing credited as a half-hand of maintenance labor every morning since the night of the stay, matched by a district form number issued to more than one office.

## Chapter 0023 — The Name on the Load

Meridith Vale enters Ilyan's name in the authority row of the public work slate under a four-year-old standing instruction, and Oren refuses to let the guild work under another body's authority. A loaded cart reads the board, changes its line, takes the eastern turn fast, and breaks the approach apron; the name is not a force on the stone but a force on the people who read it before they reach it. A guild apprentice refuses to read the scale aloud while that name is on the board, and Oren takes the book and signs beside him. Sera compares the slate's hand with the composite petition's hand and finds they are not the same. The slate's back shows a case copy routed through the plate office that no instruction authorizes.

## Chapter 0024 — The Assembly’s Limited Word

The Lower Ward Assembly can answer one question: may the attached name be cited as authority for local maintenance work? The district invokes the Bench's own refusal to hold enforcement in the Saltwheel matter and declines to hold the materials store. Maren Kett argues for one legible rule with no roof, and Sera says a rule that tells a clerk who pays does not tell a person she may say no. Ilyan reaches for a general rule about acknowledgment inferred from use and Sera stops him in the middle aisle in front of the hall, because his standing is one bridge and he has no facts about four districts. The assembly's limited word makes his name citable as a record, uncitable as a permit, and orders him to give a public account of his first bridge testimony in three days.

## Chapter 0025 — Ilyan’s First Public Testimony

Neris and Tamsin do not come and send no message. Ilyan answers for what he accepted, what he did not accept, and where his plan failed, including filing Neris's private flood account without asking. Asked to affirm that the stay authorized local adoption, he refuses, and then confesses to the limit he never asked to have removed: every term in the stay he accepted was a limit on the court, and nothing in it stopped a clerk from reusing its shape, and he was relieved not to have to ask for a charter amendment. A question about the pre-arrival paper forces him to produce his Earth reproduction sequence again, permanently and publicly. The lowland conservancy withdraws its citation. Afterward Sera finds that the district's working schedule book has a page cut from the binding gutter and a replaced column that was cut from a printed form rather than written by hand.

## Chapter 0026 — Sera Draws a Boundary

Ilyan wants to file to reopen the bridge record on the C—2 evidence. Sera refuses, because a reopening suspends every relief entered under the record, including the stay, and the stay is the only lawful crossing Tamsin has. She refuses to be the lever her family's missing page becomes, accepts Ilyan only as a record-checker, and gives him one task: verify ordinary custody, not a villain. They trace the printed form to a public print run, a standing print authority, and a routine maintenance update in a strongbox in a rented room — nine ordinary steps. The alteration's routing carries an incomplete code, its second element cut away.

## Chapter 0027 — The Witness Who Refuses the Schedule

Ilsa Bray refuses to sign a forward schedule reading *until reviewed and thereafter* with a wage line that has no hours, and offers the physical defect instead: on the third run the west joint comes back to its mark, walks two fingers out, and comes back. Tarin refuses to let a worker's refusal become a switch, and Ilyan files the smallest request of the batch — one span, one joint, named hands, paid, ending at the seventh morning bell. Oren records the offer and the refusal in the guild's own book. Ilsa reveals that the sealed plate's cut edge is a load-book edge, that a crew kept two witnesses, and that a woman paid off the book when the crew list was cut to one name said *a book without a second hand is only a record.* She does not claim that woman was her grandmother, and she asks the room to stop her deciding whether the levy ledger's cut name and the plate's cut are one hand.

## Chapter 0028 — The Two Books

Nine slots with no slack in them: four guild hands, three road-party hands of whom two cannot leave the road, and two ward hands, one of whom holds the whistle. Ilyan writes a second rule he has never said to anybody, does not file it, and does not destroy it, after Oren shows him that a rule making refusal free also makes refusal lawful on the worst day in the worst weather. The work is done: apron, joint course, brace, count, salt crack. The district's new two-book ledger puts paid work in one book and refusals in the other, and Vale enters the household's refusal under a position heading until Tarin points at the fourth column. Sera returns with the print shop's answer: the authorization for run 114 was cut out of the stock ledger and replaced by one signed by a preservation authority rather than a person. The form's print strip shows the run was distributed to a plate office six days before the ninth-bridge public notice.

## Chapter 0029 — The Review That Cannot Convene

The local-institution review cannot convene because the ward has no recognized address, so the ward seats are empty, and Ilyan refuses to fill them by treating the ward as one claimant. Tarin offers a neutral boundary without an order, and the sitting becomes a bounded evidence session with no findings and no order. Sera refuses in public to let the pre-arrival paper be submitted; Ilyan enters the routing as a fact instead. The quorum clerk's shelf index reveals that the bridge's maintenance-review condition was rerouted to the C—2 continuity desk on the day the working plate was executed, and that the desk has no named clerk and no incoming index.

## Chapter 0030 — The Road Beneath the Charter

The district offers the household four concessions for one maintenance signature; Neris refuses, because the charter has no exit and a signature would bind her to a rule she could never leave. The same acknowledgment is served on the guild's crew, assigning the burden to their successors until the charter is amended, and Oren refuses it on the guild's own ground and without Ilyan's record: the guild will not carry a term it cannot use. Ilyan files one request, to record the local arrangement as found and to state that the charter is not amended; appeal, enforceable exit, and durable housing and work protection are named as unresolved. Tamsin asks for one line in the file that goes to the review: *I am not a reason for the bridge.* A comparison of the sealed plate against the older witness index shows one cut and not three — same hand, tool, sitting and week, with the salt gone white in both — and the pre-arrival paper's receipt falls inside the C—2 index's gap.

## Chapter 0031 — The Review That Opens With a Number

The seventh morning bell rings at noon and the review opens on a measurement, not an argument: the court record hand Roke enters the joint's deviation and the deck line's permanent set as two different numbers because Ilsa says they are two different numbers. The bench levy is spent as of Thursday. The court attempts to close the matter under the emergency continuity rule, consolidating it into a docket with no date on the ground that the maintenance-review condition's routing is under question and a consolidated matter cannot receive new local evidence, which would have lapsed the stay at noon by the court's own procedural convenience. The court then continues the relief on its own ground — a relief does not lapse because the court continued the matter — on the identical principle the district has been using against the household, sides turned round, and the district objects and is right. Ilyan's smallest request is a pause route: one named keeper, one named inspection, a stated number of bells, no charter, no rule, no precedent, no reopening. He admits in public that he has no good answer to who pays for a stopped span, and the district immediately names the carts as the affected party, which is correct and which removes the ward and the household from the column. The review records the two-finger drift as a measured condition and orders the guild to bring a costed pause proposal to a full hearing that is not scheduled, which only an attached record-holder with standing may ask for. Nobody can refute the district's objection that asking for a scheduled hearing is a reopening, because the difference has never been tested in a public room. Bram Orren, a bell-runner, rings the change and asks for the figures for his line book and is refused.

## Chapter 0032 — The Work the Levy Does Not Pay

The guild's maintenance fund is empty and the board says so. Oren refuses to let Ilyan under the span and Ilsa refuses a stranger near the scale, so Ilyan documents instead of examining. The four candidates are the joint, the apron, the water, and the load order; the apron and the order are eliminated on the day. Bram Orren, a seventeen-year-old bell-runner from the Briar March, brings an oiled-cloth map drawn from fourteen local children and a footman at the salt wharf showing the channel moved west and the western abutment stands in its bed, and states his own want — a closed span breaks four standing arrangements on his line for people who cannot afford a court. Puck goes under the seat with a lamp and the guild finds the cause: the west seat is bedded on a course the water is washing the fines out of, so the joint is not wearing but emptying, and it borrows two fingers on load and gets them back. The fix is mundane, expensive, and needs five things the district and the guild do not have. The guild's own account shows that a crew of six at every change would have caught it four days ago, because the under-deck watch has been one hand since the year the crew list went to one name, and two hands went when the second witness went. Oren writes *a book without a second hand is only a record* on the board under everything.

## Chapter 0033 — An Institution Wins Something

In the district's rented office off the weighbridge, Oren refuses the maintenance acknowledgment and asks for nothing but the guild's own wage: two loads of crushed stone, four sacks of lime, fiber delivered to the line, and one load-held day with six hands at the levy rate, argued entirely from the guild's own board and the guild's own six-year failure to find an emptied seat. The district's position is coherent and not corrupt — a span that cannot be stopped is cheaper to maintain than one that can — and Dain says plainly that a district cannot fund a power it has neither created nor voted for. Vale supplies the office's own four-word limit: *the office has no power here.* The district concedes because declining would require its clerk to certify in writing that a span with an emptied seat is fit for unlimited load, and Vale enters that true reason on the back of the slip in his own hand. Ilyan contributes nothing and is told so; Dain tells him the object of his eleven-day objection won an argument in a room where nobody needed him. The district's price is a regional maintenance conference, convened overnight at the old weighhouse, on an agenda printed before anyone had said the word *day* out loud.

## Chapter 0034 — The Room Where the Clause Might Be Written

At the fen crossing, forty-one delegates want one sentence, and Ottla Prenn writes eleven lines that actually contain a keeper's pause. The common rule cannot be adopted because two districts cannot produce their maintenance terms, and the conference's own custody index shows both held at a code with no office written against it — the C—2 code — which is procedural failure, not moral failure, and which nobody expected. Ilyan, barred from being cited, is useful as a documentarian, warns the room that an accurate sentence gets used for whatever the reader has, and is ignored. He is then offered the only job in the room that consists of being the name on everything — keeper of a regional witness list, safe because his name cannot be cited — and refuses, correctly, on two grounds: a post with no door is a charter with no door, and a witness list must be carried by something boring, local, paid, and impossible to promote. The guild refuses it from the other side. Draft eleven circulates unadopted with the witness question open, and Oren learns that his clause is a local detail beside a region that cannot find two of its own terms.

## Chapter 0035 — The Ro Household Under the Copy

Bevin Larch delivers a notice in the lane: the Ro household is entered in the ward's common register as a participating party, pending verification, which means the district may consult it and notify it of load-pattern changes without asking. The register's own draft carried the household in because an attached record's public account was entered as a district docket — an institution responding to a record, not to a person — and the district's complaint that four records are four doors has become a gate. Tamsin asks what participation gets her (notice) and what it costs (her mother's consent to be asked, which the district no longer needs) and then asks what she must do to get out, and learns there is an objection column and no column for leaving. Ilyan asks the Bench to hold the entry; the district cites the Saltwheel distinction that Ilyan himself established and wins, and he says out loud in the lane that he taught it. Hanne Kolm reads the register draft aloud and delivers the chapter's line: a book with seven columns and no eighth is a book where people may say no and not go. Larch enters the household's objection in his own hand and appends, on his own authority, that the register records no consent and has no column for it.

## Chapter 0036 — What the Household Will Not Say

Tamsin says, unprompted, what the bell *is* rather than what it meant: a rope over a worn wheel on a pin and a hammer in a bell, fixed to the post and to nothing on the span, rung by a person. It rang, the people on the span got off, they got off, and the span went on. Tarin refuses, publicly and by name, to let the account be taken as a witness to Ilyan's theory, and Ilyan does not reach for his book. Neris refuses to send her, and gives no reason in public. Tamsin sets five terms, the fifth being that her account may not be used to cure or support any refusal, and chooses the guild's book over the court's because a book somebody wants cannot be said to have been made for her. Oren takes the account and the terms, Ilsa reads it back once and is corrected once, Neris witnesses without signing, and Oren records that the guild is not a neutral body. In the lane, Neris tells Ilyan the reason she will not explain in a room: an inspector sat on her bench on the eighth day and explained that if Tamsin said the bell was not answered, the household would not be refusing maintenance — the district was offering her daughter as a reason.

## Chapter 0037 — The Clerk Who Was Sent to Enter It

The print shop answers one question a day; Sera's is spent and Ilyan's has never been asked. Neral gets them into the cold index room behind the shop and identifies the mechanics of the cut authorization page: the shop does not re-cut stock, a bound volume is rebound by a binder, and the paste is cold, so the volume went back into service the same day. The answer is ordinary and damning: run 114 was authorized by a standing authority of preservation, which is a paper that keeps deciding the same thing while the emergency it was written for lasts and does not need a name because it was never meant to be argued with. Its index for the week the current working plate was executed shows eleven items taken into preservation, all routed C—2, two named in class: the ninth-bridge maintenance-review condition and a sealed continuity request. Neither carries a name. Sera declines to ask what is in the sealed item and the desk records the refusal. Ilyan's temptation is to build a villain out of an office; Vale names the price — records stop coming, to everybody — and redirects him to the wage. Vale also tells him his standing has lapsed at the bell. The day ends with an automatic district notice: the refused acknowledgment must be re-issued or the schedule lapses at dusk, and Oren tells Ilyan he does not write this next arrangement.

## Chapter 0038 — The Schedule Lapses

At dusk the district's schedule ends on its own term with no breach by anybody, entered in three short true lines by Vale, and the stone sits at a yard at the district's expense with no lawful reason to move it. Oren refuses Ilyan the pen and the bodies that will carry the work write the arrangement themselves: the guild's empty account, the road party, the pump's own take at the ward rate with Dalla Rusk refusing to speak for the ward and entering two days of shortage in public, and an eighth of a load-fee from nine named cart owners, with no household paying anything. It is better than the last two arrangements — every hour named, refusals still refusals — and worse in a named way: there is no district copy any more, and a carrier will not put a fen-road roof load on a road that ends on a date with nothing behind it, so the lower road's shelter loses its roof. Neris names the household that loses it first: Marda Rennick, room nine, and her son Besh, nine, who cannot be on a ladder. Bram's line book is the only record of what actually crossed. Larch enters the cause of the lapse in a public ward roll because a record that does not say why is a record somebody can be frightened by, and Ilyan says the plain sentence that the lapse is the district's decision, and nobody argues.

## Chapter 0039 — The Continuity Voice in a Public Field

Harrow returns, reads the file standing in the cold, confirms the scheduling request is built out of four things and no archive, and then states that Ilyan's standing has lapsed, which means the court cannot take the request from him. The guild asks instead, in the name of a burden named in the record, on a slate Oren holds up; that is the batch's loss producing its win. Dain objects that a scheduling upon a consolidated matter is a reopening; Harrow opens the field on five disputed records plus the guild's working count book, at Tarin's insistence. The joint goes out three fingers and back, a figure nobody has seen, while the permanent set does not move. A fifth mark appears in the count book's margin — a count-book cut for a load that walked and did not come back — with nobody near the book. Tarin walks under the rope, picks it up with both hands, refuses Dain's attempt to secure it, and holds it until a court clerk's time and his own signature record what he is doing, because he once enforced an order on a village on the strength of a record he did not test. The Bench's ruling schedules the full hearing for the fourth morning bell and does not reopen the record, and carries beneath it a clipped instruction nobody requested: *continuity preserves the record before any remedy is stated.* Roke says it is not on his tray. Harrow refuses to rule on it, on the ground that a court which cannot say what a line in its own translation is has no business amending a charter. Ilyan refuses to build on it in public because he cannot say who said it, and Sera refuses in the same breath to let the Quiet Chamber chain be used to trace it.

## Chapter 0040 — What the Field Left Standing

Ilyan's four load-bearing facts are read aloud in the workroom and Sera corrects two of the four and is right about exactly those two: the drift is a band (one and a half on a light load, up to three on a heavy one) and not a figure, and the schedule lapsed because a term ran out on an instrument nobody re-issued, not because the guild refused. Moot enters, in the open, that the attached record-holder has no remaining constituency: the conservancy withdrawn, the name uncitable, the household a party under objection, the ward seats unfilled, the standing lapsed, and one preserved attached observation that binds nobody. Ilyan delegates in daylight — Oren takes the physical witness and will describe rather than advocate, Ilsa takes the measurement and keeps the half, Joren takes the road and the load sequence, Tarin takes the boundary and will testify to what a field does not write, Bram's line book is named and refused before it can be used — and keeps only the record, saying that if a court finds a fifth thing he will not have been the one looking for it. Tamsin consents to a copy rather than the book on four terms of her own, including that the court must say out loud that the account went to the guild first and nobody made her; Neris reserves the right to stop everything herself if her daughter is put in a room. Sera names the one lever left in the case, the sealed item in the preservation index, and forbids it, because nobody knows what happens to an item a court has ruled on. The batch closes on a span with a chalked end date, a measured and funded but unfixed drift, an unexplained mark in a guild's book, a set hearing, and a C—2 chain one step deeper with no name in it.

## Chapter 0041 — The Hours the Arrangement Does Not Have

The last evening of the Chapter 38 arrangement. The last day of a week is a thin road: nine carts over, seven loaded, the four empties gone east and not coming back, so the load-fee is short and A. wants the frame to say so. Oren's crew works it on the guild's own empty account; Puck comes up out of the hatch with grey lamp glass and reports that there is no stone in the bed of the west course, only a skin of lime, and that he is not a measure. Neris dictates the exact sentence Ilyan must say to Marda Rennick about the roof, and forbids the word *carrier* in front of her son. Oren costs a pause for the first time — two bells for a load that can turn, six for one that must be reversed with a full approach behind it, eight as the ceiling, six hands, at the ward rate — and the sum is unaffordable, and he says the true reason: a clause nobody pays for is a bell nobody rings. Dain then produces the district's answer he has been carrying since the fourth day: the district will carry the hour, not the power, will pay the stopped hour and restore the under-deck watch to two hands, and will not fund a rule by habit. Ilsa reframes the whole question: the bell has never held a load, it empties the deck, so a keeper's power cannot be bigger than the machine it is rung on. Larch reads the district's roll at the rope: the answer is accepted, and it is arithmetically impossible, because a ward rate may be paid only to a body with a name and the ward has no name.

## Chapter 0042 — The Book Nobody Had Sworn

The small hours, in the shelter on the lower road. Ilyan says the sentence Neris wrote for him, word for word, and will not say *carrier*. Marda Rennick asks the useful question — is it coming back — and Besh will be nine for a long time yet. Hanne Kolm brings twelve years of unsworn landlord's book and the reason she has never sworn it: names in a sworn book can be used. Ilyan names the household's three asks — a crossing, a room, a refusal — and says out loud, before anyone finds out in a court, that he cannot read the machinery of the tenancy. Tamsin, who has not been asleep, works out from her own second term that if she is to speak it must be with the span in view, and chooses the span, the rope, the machine, and the fourth condition: the court must say out loud that the account went to the guild first and nobody made her. The walk up at a quarter to the fourth bell; a carter has been calling Ilyan *Sedge* for eighteen days because the board's columns are read at a canter; Oren rings the bell for the change and the fourth morning bell goes and the arrangement ends. Harrow opens the full hearing with a new rule of his own: the burden of each remedy will be stated by the party objecting to it.

## Chapter 0043 — The Three, Said Out Loud Before the Argument

Harrow's innovation works. Kett argues the amendment for one legible rule and is undone by his own honesty — when asked who pays the undefined burden he says *I cannot name them* and asks that it be recorded. Dain argues for breaking the span and makes the district pay for the argument: break it only if every household that loses the crossing is given a named route and a named cost first, and the fourth bridge is a toll bridge with a toll the district cannot move, and he puts the toll in his own mouth on purpose. Neris asks for it read back to her. Joren argues for holding it open and loses on the strongest ground in the case: the court's own refusal of it is that a cost which is not a number cannot be carried by a woman on a lower road for a fourth time. Harrow then requires a physical witness: four loads, a count read aloud, and the bell rung in front of the court so it can hear what a warning sounds like before it writes a word about one.

## Chapter 0044 — What the Span Did While the Court Was Arguing

Puck goes down at the drain side and puts a rule seven inches into the west course: the stone is gone, the lime is a skin, the water comes up four inches in a day. The count runs, and Coll writes the word *nothing* in a count book, which is the first entry of its kind and the most useful. Then a salt cart is set on the west end with a full approach behind it. Ilsa reads out three and back and back — and it stops a full finger out. The pale permanent set has not moved; Oren says so out loud for the record. It is the first time the joint has ever failed to return. Oren rings the bell, the whole court gets off the deck including Harrow with the tray over his head, and the salt cart stays on the empty seat with nobody on it: a released span with a load on it. Harrow asks Ilyan what the court is looking at; Ilyan answers the physical fact and then says outright that he cannot give the rule that makes the number go where it goes, and Harrow writes it down himself. One interval's recess.

## Chapter 0045 — The Money With Nowhere to Go

The recess is working work. The salt cart comes off in nine minutes and the joint returns all the way. Puck prices the re-bedding: four props, six to eight shifts, five tides against you in fourteen days, taken from the fourth bridge. Ilyan admits, in front of sixty people, that he can read Puck's numbers and not the work behind them. Dalla Rusk supplies the answer nobody had written down — the ward rate is a number, not a recipient, so pay the person at the moment of the work. Vale comes down the steps nineteen minutes early, pays the pump's shortage, and then enters the reason the district cannot do what it has just offered: a ward rate may be paid only to a body with a name, and the ward is not a body, and that is scar tissue from a year when naming a ward was used to take a road off people. He enters that the man who built the register gate is the man who cannot pay the toll, in his own hand, with the hour. The court's system entry records the payments and the impossibility, and the office's limit, and nothing is a remedy.

## Chapter 0046 — The Column That Is Not a Name

Harrow's question is what a document can look like so a duty is payable without writing the thing that takes the address away again. Dalla's answer is adopted: the ward rate is paid to the person, at the moment of the work, by name, on a day-book line, in chalk. Then Hanne Kolm walks forty minutes with the box and refuses to swear the tenancy book, and swears a different book — one about burdens, with names in it and no addresses in it — and asks for it as a column and not a book, because a book is a thing you can lose and a column is a thing you have to look at. The four things the column records: burden refused, who carried it instead, date, what it did to the span. The register has a column for objection and no column for leaving, and that is the same act as a signature. The guild's four props go to the ninth bridge and the fourth bridge's keeper, Wist Anhalt, sends four words: the props are yours, I object. The roof is scheduled for the last morning the span is open, on six pairs of hands at the district's expense — and Joren refuses to write a roof into a bridge document so that a roof looks as though it came from a bridge. Tamsin walks onto the span, asks not to be called, and pulls the rope once so that everybody knows which rope she means.

## Chapter 0047 — The Account of a Machine

Tamsin gives the account on the span, in daylight, with the rope in front of her, and it is a machine and not a meaning: rope, wheel, worn pin, hammer, bell, and the post, and nothing on the span. Harrow reads the five terms and lets a hard question go on purpose, and says so. Dain then makes a procedural argument that is entirely correct: the account is evidence that a known condition was unanswerable, and a maintenance acknowledgment would be a recognition rather than an imposition. Sera reads the fifth term aloud and refuses the use; the acknowledgment dies; Dain's observation and objection are both entered, and he asks the district's gap be entered too — the wages imply a recognition the household has not given. Tamsin then supplies the part nobody asked for: the pin is worn where nobody can see it, the post stands on the west side over the hole, and a person pulling a slack rope is a person pulling a rope. The bell's wheel and pin are entered as span maintenance, inspected on a named day by two hands, one of whom has pulled the rope.

## Chapter 0048 — The Text on the Table, and What It Cost to Put It There

The court sits into the night by lamplight and the last day of the week runs out unnoticed. Oren reads the guild's costed proposal into the record and then signs a maintenance acknowledgment — the one he refused — in his own name and not six crews', accepting the work unpaid, the bell's pin, and a term that ends with the keeper. Odile reads the draft charter in full court register; Ilyan loses the thread at the enumerated cases and says so in front of three hundred people, and Harrow enters it, because a precedent that hides the fact that its author cannot read it is a precedent that will be used against him. Sera has a read-back in the guild's words filed beside the instrument, and the sentence that makes it work: a keeper may not stop a load, and what a keeper may do is call the deck clear, and the deck being clear is the whole of it. Sera is formally licensed as a recordwright of a public record and not as Ilyan's advocate, on four terms, and says what it costs her: her shop, her rooms, the business that has been carrying her family's unfiled claim for six years, and the right to withdraw quietly. At the last bell of the night she enters the settlement in the Quiet Chamber and finds one surviving line on the facing leaf of the cut page — *a refusal is entered, and the entry is against the burden and not against the person, and the entry is open to the ward* — and declines to guess at the shadow of the rest. In a corridor she tells Ilyan to be frightened of the difference between a precedent and a template.

## Chapter 0049 — The Ruling

Harrow disposes of all three remedies out loud, refused ones first. Hold is refused because an interval is what a room gives itself when it does not have a ruling, and Joren's objection is preserved. Break is refused because Dain's own condition — a named crossing, a sworn tenancy, a protected water route, before anything — has been met, and the district's objection is preserved and expressly invited to appeal. Amend is adopted, with ten terms: the maintenance-review condition restored with its original wording expressly *not* recovered; the bell's wheel and pin inspected by two hands; the whole maintenance burden named, guild and district, including the fourth bridge's props and the objection of a keeper who is not a party; the ward rate paid to the person at the moment of the work; a pause that lasts as long as the loads standing and no longer; an appeal that does not suspend access, housing, or work, with the court's own seventh-morning-bell ground written into the charter; an exit, with the refusal entered against a burden and not against a person; access, housing, and work, including that an unlisted person is not a trespasser by that fact alone; the guild's acknowledgment, ending with its keeper; a public review that may be called early and not postponed. Ilyan's name goes on the precedent, at his own request and with no reason given. Kett objects to the phrase *lawful traveler* and is told the phrase is the charter's own word and that the instrument will be read with the frame. Beneath the ruling the continuity line appears a fifth time, with *stated* changed to *entered*, and Roke says it is not on his tray, and Harrow refuses to rule on it for the third time and says why. The district's day-book line is opened in chalk on the frame and the first wage is paid, by name, to six hands for a two-bell pause.

## Chapter 0050 — The Line Beside the Line

Ten days on, the span is propped over the channel and carries nothing, and the count still runs with two hands under the deck because the second hand is on the ward's line. The roof goes over on the morning of the ruling, the last morning the span is open, on nine bundles and six pairs of hands at the district's expense, and room nine is done first because a woman who cannot go up a ladder is not a woman who can wait two days; Besh goes up the outside stair and is entered as a safety incident and describes it as *the bit where I did the thing*; and Tamsin sends Ilyan away from the lane because he has not got a roof to put on. A rider brings a two-day-old print from Halloway, a poor district on the low water with a sanitation complaint in front of its bench: the same words, in the same order, in a different column, and *lawful traveler* doing the opposite work. Kett will go and stand in their room under protest. Then the strip of board goes up beside the old lawful-traveler register — Hanne Kolm's sworn column, a record of crossing and not a register, with a child's line reading AND NAMES under it — and the twenty-third name on it is Halide Sen, who walked eleven miles from a district three valleys away because somebody on a low-water culvert had told her the ninth bridge was open to people who are not on a list. She crosses. Sera closes the ledger with the appeal date and the appeal's non-suspension of access on the inside of the back cover, and says what the license is and is not, and that if it becomes affection it will be a separate entry and it will be dated. Far off, past four bridges and a district that printed a word it did not understand, another span takes a load at dusk and answers with one clear note.

# Volume 02 Chapter Summaries

## Chapter 0051 — The Gate at the Low Water

Ilyan walks the four miles down to Halloway's low water on the twelfth day of the unloaded period and does not get into the room, because his name is on the paper and every person there would read the name and stop listening. **The seven days between the copy and the walk are on the page: the copy up the fen road on the fifth logged sideways into the ninth-bridge matter because no file had Halloway's name on it, read twice on the frame against the settlement with the same answer both times — lawful, made out of a public instrument, carrying the sentence that helps a person and not the one that would have cost the district something — and nothing he can do with it, because his own name is the reason; the court's attachment on the ninth; the carter who was turned back coming up off the fen road empty at the second bell on the tenth, unable to say which gate and unasked in a district room; and the choice of the twelfth over the fourteenth because on the twelfth he can stand in a lane and be nobody for a whole day.** On the bank he counts a standpipe queue of forty-one at the sixth bell and knows there will be nine at the ninth, and he reads a sheet that was printed nine days ago, nailed up with four nails and no glass, in which the fourth paragraph turns the ninth bridge's own two-hundred-year-old word *lawful traveler* into a reason to shut a gate, against a traveller book four inches thick and a hundred years out of date. **Wenna Tace, twenty-nine, a refuse hauler and the only person on her street who reads, reads the sheet out at the queue and reads the fourth paragraph twice, which she has never done in eleven years, and says the word in it is the bridge word and that it means a person in a book.** A ratepayer asks whether the sheet is his; Ilyan says it is an extract of a settlement he asked to be citable and did not send, and that nobody had been cruel to produce it. Bryn Sallow, forty-four, gate hand for thirty years, says the sheet is not wrong — she is the one who is wrong, because she does not know a word on it and nobody has told her in thirty years which of two things is true, and asks for it to be written down, and it is entered in the district's day book in the clerk's own hand. Wenna asks the question nobody can answer: she is not on the book, she hauls two hundred loads a year out of the low water, and *am I lawful.* Corva Silt, thirty-one, the bench clerk, admits before anybody else that she printed the extract on the second day after the hearing under a lawful print authority and sent a copy to the man whose name is at the bottom of it. Kett's written instruction is produced and read at his request, *one legible rule, without a roof*, and he asks that the record show that its second half was not written until a sheet was already on a gate. **The chapter closes in the room off the basalt court on the ninth, three days earlier, where Harrow attaches the record-holder to the fourteenth in Halloway as a witness to his own observation and to nothing else, and says it is a fact and not a punishment and that he is sorry about the corridor and not about the rule.** Sera says she will come, and that coming is not the same as being on his side.

## Chapter 0052 — The First Honest Use

On the thirteenth day the water is let at the second bell and the room empties itself, and a licensed recordwright arrives at the fifth bell with the ninth-bridge read-back in the guild's words and says in the first minute that she is not a witness for Ilyan and that Halloway has not asked. She reads nine sentences, and the room does not obey them — it finds the one that gets somebody something. **Wenna Tace gets eleven months of arrears out of a bench minute by pointing at the sentence that says a person not on a register is not a trespasser by that fact alone, and the hundred and fifty hauls in the same eleven months that are in no book of the district cannot be entered by anybody in the room, because nobody there can say what a haul is where there is no cart.** Bryn Sallow tries the same sentence and it does not work, because it is about a span, and a span has a deck and a load path and a keeper, and a culvert gate has none of those things. Somebody says *then get it changed*, and Kett explains that the ninth bridge cannot write a district's lawful-traveler definition and the district's own register is its own instrument. Ilyan's whole contribution is four numbers off a slate — forty-one at the sixth bell, nine at the ninth, nineteen at the seventh, eleven carts turned back, four loads of district sand gone back up the hill — and the observation that the water is rationed by a shift and not by a notice, and the refusal to say what the bench may do, on the ground that a foreign record-holder does not get to tell a poor district what its notice may say. Corva Silt then admits that she read the fifth paragraph of her own instrument once at night on a lamp, that it said a ward rate paid to a person at the moment of the work in chalk on a day-book line, and that this district has no ward and has a woman, and that she is going to print a form of the district's own on the fourteenth, numbered, because a copy is somebody else's mistake and a numbered instrument can be numbered again.

## Chapter 0053 — The Form

The fourteenth day. At the fifth bell Ilyan is in the room on the strength of the Bench's order, and Corva Silt has Instrument H-9 on the table under her hand: five clauses, numbered, sealed, published at the fourth bell. Wenna Tace reads the first clause twice on her own initiative and will not be asked for it twice, because the word in it is the one they will all argue about, and then reads on. **Clause three is the best clause on the instrument and the clerk wrote it in the small hours because a notice that says who may open a gate and does not say it is the gate hand is a notice that says anybody may open a gate.** The turn is not a speech and not a clause: it is the cart coming back down the hill in front of eighty people with everything still on it. **Abel Kearn, thirty-five, a builder out of the upper street with a builder's apron and lime in the seam of it,** asks what Ilyan did about this and gets the true answer, that he asked in a corridor for the settlement to be citable, did not send it, and was not frightened, and that he cannot read a clause and has said so on the record, and that if anybody wants to make a rule about that they should make it about him and not about the paper — and lands the sharpest line in the room: *then you are no use in this room, and I am sorry, because I have a bad thing and I want somebody to make it good, and there is no somebody.* **He also names the whole shape of it, that there is one lawyer's clerk in this district, she is thirty-one, and she printed it.** Kett finds that Halloway has used the bridge's paper to say something true — its own hundred-and-sixty-year-old book calls the culvert road a penal road — and calls the template a *frame* rather than a monster, and says he does not know whether to be proud of the rule or sorry for the paper. Ilyan gives them the numbers and refuses to give them a ruling. **At the sixth bell, in front of eighty people, Bryn Sallow rings a handbell on a chain — attached to nothing — and a carter takes four loads of district sand and lime back up the fen road, and the clerk enters, at Ilyan's request and not the bench's, that the gate hand was not instructed and that no person instructed the gate hand.** The convenor closes by saying the thing nobody has said: if somebody had come down that bank on the second day with a form and said *this closes your gate, and it is right, and I am sorry*, she would have been glad of it, and somebody might have come.

## Chapter 0054 — The Name on the Bottom of the Paper

The fifteenth day. Cael Rundle, fifty-eight, ratepayer of Weir Lane, moves that the ninth-bridge settlement be withdrawn from use in Halloway — not the sheet, the precedent — because it produced H-9, and says he is not asking for the water. Ilyan is produced as the record-holder and asked the fair question: does your own paper, which says a person not on a register is not a trespasser by that fact alone, make his gate unlawful. **It is the fairest question anyone has asked him since he crossed and he does not have the answer, and he says so standing up in front of sixty people in a room where he has no standing, no constituency, and no advocate, and gives the reasons in order — a span has a deck and a load path and a keeper, and a culvert gate has none of those things, and the difference is in the machinery of a legal instrument that nobody in the district has.** He puts the cost on the table before anybody finds it: the answer is four miles up the road, the sixth-bell queue is on the bank, the gate is worked by one woman who cannot sit down, and **there is no line in this district on which her hour can be paid, because the line that pays an hour like that is a term of the paper the ratepayer has just asked to withdraw.** Bryn Sallow prices it at an hour and Corva Silt enters it and finds there is no line to put it on, and writes in the district's own hand that the office has no line. The answer arrives at the ninth bell in a counter's hand off the bridge, in four lines, ending *I am not a lawyer and I count loads*: the access condition is about this span, a span has a deck and a load path and a keeper, a gate on a culvert has none of those things, and a district may make a list of its own. Cael does not withdraw his motion, and asks for something else instead: a line in this district's book on which an hour of work can be paid to a person by name, and a date. His daughter of fourteen asks him every morning whether she is a trespasser. Corva Silt strikes out clause two of her own instrument and insists the bench minute it formally so it is not hers alone. Kett says the request is going up the road, and Sera says she is going to carry the terms to the weighhouse because it is the third of the four things her license says she does, and is going to tell a magistrate that a poor district's bench has done more honest administration in four days than she has seen in a month.

## Chapter 0055 — One Legible Rule

The eighteenth day, and Orison is a place for the first time. The First Verdict is a block of black basalt the size of a house with no throne and no roof, and the scarce thing at it is a place on a docket: nineteen names on the board, eleven places, and the eleven are all bridge matters. A licensed recordwright and the court translator read the ninth-bridge conditions together all morning without once disagreeing about a word, which happens about four times a year and did not happen at all in the first month of the case. **Magistrate Ansa Rell, forty-four, eleven years in the office, calls the common-rule sitting, requires the burden to be stated before the opinion, borrows Harrow's procedure and says where she got it, and puts a man who spoke from a gallery back on the floor.** Convener Bel Ordway, who has a salt cart on the weighbridge and four minutes of the hour, says he wants one legible rule because he is tired of his own clerk. **Ansel Nyle, fifty-one, keeper of the low fen's one bridge and in the post since he was nineteen, produces his district's maintenance terms: they adopted the ninth-bridge settlement entire on the fifth day, by copying a public instrument out of a book, and his keeper has called the deck clear twice and both peat carts that could not turn were off the deck in nine minutes and both were paid out of road funds to the person on a line in chalk. The ward line does not go short on a thin week, because the ward line comes out of road funds and the toll does not.** Ordwin Sarn, of the cut banks, says the same and adds that his clerk cried about the water on the seventh day and that is normal and is the only part of this nobody warned her about. **The sitting fails on a category and not on a document: a rate may be paid only to a body with a name, the ward is not a body, and two delegates in the same room name the ward differently and are each right about somebody. Ansa Rell's entered finding is that this city has two words for the same poor and will not pay either of them, and the draft is neither adopted nor withdrawn and goes to eleven benches with the ward question on its face and no answer.** She tells Ilyan, before the adjournment, that a magistrate can hear a claim about a gate and cannot hear a grievance about a wall. On the weighbridge at dusk, Sarn says that a road man came down his road a week ago with a printed form and a list of eleven dead hands, and Ilyan declines to chase it and says why: there is a preservation office in this city that has been keeping records on purpose for four years and he has not gone near it, and the man who chases every odd thing that comes up the road is the man who makes records stop coming, to everybody. He tells Sarn to write it on the scale-house wall in the hand he weighs in, not for him, for whoever asks in a year.

## Chapter 0056 — Two Shadows and a Door That Opens the Wrong Way

The nineteenth day, and a hearing field opens at the low-water culvert at the fifth bell, on the tide turn, because a field which tests records behind a bench tests nothing. Harrow says twice, on the face of the field, that the court has no power over the gate and takes no view on whether it should be open or shut. The disputed words are the ninth bridge's own sentence about a trespasser. **Ilyan sees two shadows from one body: his own on the gate leaf, and another across sixty feet of the flat with nobody standing on it, and he understands that a sentence about a person has two people in it and that a rule cannot say where the other one is.** The assembly comes up out of the flat in four seconds, legibly and without ceremony: the traveller book at the foot, the ninth bridge's own word in the middle like a pin through two boards, the district's penalty-road book as a rib, the gate hand's clause bolted through, and the load — the whole of the case — not in the document at all. The handbell's chain trembles in a wind that is not touching it four seconds before it is rung, and he feels it in his right hand, and it is the same note as a rope over a wheel on a post four miles up the road. **The foot-gate in the leaf of the gate is eighteen inches wide, hinged at the bottom, and opens outward — toward the flat, toward the people who are not on a list — so that a person on the lane side cannot use it without stepping onto the side the rule has put them on.** He practises: two documents about money, Kolm's entry and the ninth bridge's term four, in the same field; four seconds of true, legible, useful result — two documents, two people, no body in either — paid for immediately in a cold hip and six seconds of not being able to pick up a pencil. Then he uses it once in public and is wrong: *it is on her.* The field disagrees with him by putting two records beside one another, and the seam goes past Bryn Sallow, down through the shape on the flat, into a ward that has no name. His left leg is not his. Wenna Tace crosses ninety feet of flat in ninety seconds and says he has got somebody's legs, and says she has been waiting two days to say something to the court and can wait. The gate hand accounts for the foot-gate as a machine, in the past tense, twice, with no improvement: *a man with a loaded barrow cannot turn a foot-gate on a soft bank. It was not a rule. It was a hinge.* Harrow sets the burden schedule for the sixth bell the next day and tells Ilyan he will sit down for the whole of it and not read anything. On the milestone at dusk the spent blackglass token is warm for the first time in five weeks, and touching it takes the low water out of his nose for fifty minutes, and it comes back with the water over the flat and a third layer underneath, and he understands in his body that the passage that brought him and the afterimage are the same operation.

## Chapter 0057 — The Page With the Hours On It

The twentieth day. The props come out of the ninth bridge in the middle of the day, four miles away, and Ilyan finds out at the last bell and makes himself useful in the wrong place, and Coll reads back the message including the fourth bridge's four props, the same cart before the review, and Wist Anhalt's four words nailed to the frame. **The burden schedule is taken at the sixth bell, which the gate hand had asked them not to use.** Wenna Tace says the question is asked wrong: the sheet already chose a person by not choosing, because it names an office and there is one person in the office. She then says the thing the batch turns on: **if the gate hand is paid for, the wage comes off the water, off the buckets, on Weir Lane, where nobody is on the traveller book either, and it will be the first tax the people of that district have paid anybody in nineteen years, and it will have been invented by a court in Orison on the strength of a bridge.** Bryn Sallow writes her own line of the schedule and refuses the number: *from the first light on the rise to the dark on the fall, every day, by the gate hand. Not my hours. I have never counted them and I am not going to start now for a page* — because a number makes it a wage and a wage wants a rate and a rate wants a body. The schedule is four lines, the fourth of which says no rate is entered because the rate is set in a book four miles up the road that this court has not amended. It is the first page in this city's history with a woman's own hours on it, and the hours are not a number. Tarin asks the question a neutral boundary is for — four hundred and eleven loads on that flat, and who said yes to them — and Dain gives the account of a practice: a burden assigned to nobody is assigned to the person with the least standing in the room, every time, without exception, in nineteen years. Harrow refuses to treat the absence of a rate as the absence of a wage, enters the hours as hours, and sends the page to nine benches. Bryn Sallow asks for a column and not a book, in the words of a woman on a lower road who said it a week earlier and whom she has never met, and asks that the name on the line be her name and not the office. Ilyan goes up the bank and tells Wenna Tace that she did not get a rule, she got a line in a pay book, and that it was a coincidence with a name on it, and that he does not know how to make a coincidence into a rule. She tells him to go and read the trespasser sentence to a boy of nine on the bank, because a boy can tell the difference between a man doing that and a man doing the other thing.

## Chapter 0058 — A Loss Taken on the Record

The twenty-first morning, and the leg is worse and the smell is in two layers in a lane with hay in it, and Sera makes him decide in advance what he will do when it happens. **The question is one sentence: whether the ninth-bridge settlement has any force in a district that printed or quoted it without adopting it.** Kett argues the true thing — a span has a deck and a keeper and a review date, and a review date cannot be a fact about four districts at once — and Harrow holds the argument and then says what his ruling costs before he rules: the low fen and the cut banks adopted the settlement by copying a public instrument, it worked, and if the settlement does not travel their keepers have no power, their pay lines are for hours no term requires, and neither man can produce his own terms. **Ilyan's request — a named declaration that the settlement binds no district that has not adopted it — is granted in about nine minutes, and he withdraws it on the record, in four sentences, and then gets a cart down the fen road with a folded sheet in a keeper's hand: *if it binds nowhere I have a copy of nothing and a cart that cannot turn. You do not owe me the power. I want you to know that I know, and that I have had two days with it.*** He says the true reason: he made this citable in a corridor a month ago, has spent five weeks telling four districts that a remedy copied past its place becomes a different instrument, and the consequence of that sentence is that the sentence does not travel, and the day a man decides his own instrument is the exception because the exception is him, he is the thing he has been describing. What he gets instead is smaller and he says so first: that a citation is not an adoption, sent to every bench that has printed or quoted the settlement, and four hands at the gate for four hours a day for eight days at the district's own rate, by name, in chalk, with the balance of the tide entered as unpaid, and the district free to refuse. **The accumulation reaches the point where he cannot look at a seam in a room.** He goes up the bank with his hands shaking and takes the token out, and the low water goes out of his nose for fifty minutes, and comes back with the water over the flat and a layer under both that is a chain on a gate in his right hand. Ansa Rell walks into the field four days early, tells him she is going to put his withdrawn request in the same paper as a thing that has been asked for and refused, says that if he had won it then in four districts the answer to a man with a bell for the rest of his life would be *that is the ninth bridge, and the ninth bridge is the ninth bridge* — quoted, not argued with — and says he did the right thing and is not going to be thanked, because what he did was not administration but a person declining to be convenient, and there is no mechanism anywhere for that. She names the coming sitting: a clean rule that shuts the gate and gets paid for against a charter four hundred people can read and nobody can afford. Ilyan says a third thing exists, that he wrote it once, that it took a bridge and a guild and a child and a landlord and a clerk's slip, and that nobody has ever written one for a ward and he could not do it without a keeper.

## Chapter 0059 — The First Appeal, and the Woman It Does Not Reach

The field closes on the twenty-second, in nine minutes, with no finding on the gate. On the twenty-third **Wenna Tace asks whether there is a way to refuse the gate**, having worked it out for herself from a term she heard read aloud twice. The exit is entered in a register kept in the ward and sworn by the landlord of the lower road, and there is no landlord of the lower road in this district, and the instrument is four miles up the road, and she is four miles too far away, and has been for eleven years, and did not know it until the fourteenth. The appeal is worse, because it runs to any person affected by a ruling **on this span** and the culvert is not the span. Sera says both of those things out loud, in front of everybody, as her own instrument, and adds that both of the two things this city built for the person who has been harmed are on one bridge in one ward, and that a district that has not asked has no door in it, and that she went in the front and found a wall nineteen years old with a clerk in it. Harrow answers *who do I write to* himself, after letting the room go quiet on purpose, and says the only place he knows is a column on a lower road, and that a refusal does not need a court — it needs somewhere to be written down and a person who will swear to it. Cael Rundle offers his cart and will not be thanked for it. **On the twenty-third, at the ninth bell, Ilyan files the first appeal in the record on term six, before the review date, with notice to every one of the fourteen named parties and one seal, and the appeal does not suspend access, housing, or work, and the whole apparatus does exactly what it was built to do.** He enters, at Harrow's request, that this is the fourth thing in this city's record out of his own account that he cannot un-enter, after two sequences and the precedent, and says which of the four cost somebody an afternoon so that a clerk in four years does not assume they are the same size. Neris Ro gets the notice on the twenty-fourth, reads it in a lane with a basket of washing, and asks for the arithmetic to be entered while the woman who can check it is standing up. **Tamsin Ro, ten, not called and on nobody's list, comes down the fen road, asks a gate hand three questions about a chain, and comes back up the bank and says the difference between her rope and that handbell is not the machine. The difference is that somebody looked at the pin.**

## Chapter 0060 — Four Hours, and a Name on a Chalk Line

The twenty-fourth. Four paid hours a day from the fifth bell, eight days from the twenty-first, in chalk on a barrel lid at the gate because Corva Silt would not post a wage anywhere a person going for water would have to turn round to read it. The balance of the tide is entered as unpaid, and the gate hand has refused to count it. **Hanne Kolm walked the four miles on the twenty-fourth and swore a schedule to her own sworn volume at Halloway** — not a new instrument, not a new column, not a new volume, and what is new is that nobody will take it: burden refused, who carried it instead, the date, what it did, no consent, no address, and on its face that she is the landlord of a road four miles from the place it was done. **The court neither accepts nor refuses it.** Corva Silt, three days later, in a lane: it is not lawful, it is a sworn instrument sworn outside the ward by a person with no office in this district about a burden she has never held and cannot pay, and the refusal stands either way, because a refusal is exercised and is not conditional on anybody finding a column for it — *a bad instrument and a good right and both of them true.* Wenna Tace is paid on the nineteenth and says the thing that is bigger than the thing it came from, and it is entered. Cael Rundle's motion comes back on the back of a weighing-house letter from Ordwin Sarn: *in six years I have paid every one of them on my own book and I have written the hours in my own hand and a man who is dead cannot have his hours disputed.* On the twenty-sixth **Bram Orren comes down the fen road with the Bridgeward in his line book**: a toll taken for eight years on a road that is good, a woman of seventy-one called Wen Lask collecting it, a form copied term for term from the ninth bridge with eleven dead names entered against it, and no way to challenge it, because a challenge must be served on every party named and every party named is dead, and the clerk's office that took the form closed the week it went in, and the desk is a desk. He has put it in nobody's file, and says that if Ilyan goes and looks then in about a year that road will be a bad road for the only reason anybody in that family has ever heard of. Ilyan's leg holds for six days and then does not, and on the twenty-seventh it takes him off his feet, and Tarin Keel and a boy of nine carry him. On the twenty-ninth Miko Ferren, nine, asks whether he can be in the column, is told no, says he does not want to be, and goes to check whether the bell on the bridge post is the same as the one on the gate. The leg goes again at the fourth bell. The wages ended on the twenty-eighth, six and a half hours a day short of a working tide of ten and a half, fifty-two hours of unpaid balance across the eight days, and Oakes refused them in writing on the ground that **a district that pays a favour is a district that has learned to rule by habit**, which is the ninth bridge's tenth term, in three places at once, and the sentence this whole business turns on. Ansa Rell's clerk brings a docket for the twenty-ninth: the court can write a clean instrument in an afternoon and cannot write the other thing; four benches have tried in nineteen years and produced eleven valid instruments and none that was affordable, and the court is afraid; and the missing thing is not a rule and not a remedy but **a piece of paper that a person can swear to in a room with no address in it, and no bench in this city holds one that travels.** Sera reads the facing leaf again and finds that *open to the ward* does not say the ward's clerk holds it, and says she would rather be wrong in a lane than be wrong in a record. The count comes back at mark on a span with nothing on it, and on the last light load it walked out one and a half and came back all the way, and the pale permanent set does not move, and the bell is rung for the change by a keeper who has looked at the pin by two people, one of whom has pulled the rope. Four miles down the fen road, a name in chalk and a figure beside it, and the reason for the ending on the back of the board, where people read it going for water.

## Chapter 0061 — The Word in the Guild's Book
